4264 20
4265 6
4266 Sage of the Wilds
4268 Hit Points Hit Dice: 1d8 per Minstrel level.
4269 Proficiencies Armor: Light armor, medium armor, shields
4270 Weapons: Simple weapons, scimitars, longbows
4271 Saving Throws: Intelligence, Wisdom
4272 Skills: Choose two from Animal Handling, Arcana, Craft (Alchemy, Herbal, Poison), History, Insight, Medicine, Nature, Perception, Performance, Religion, and Survival.
4273 Equipment
4274 a wooden shield and a simple melee weapon or (b) a longbow and 20 arrows
4275 a scimitar or (b) any simple melee weapon
4276 an explorer's pack or (b) a priest's pack
4277 leather armor or (b) hide armor
4278 Beast Speech
4279 Starting at 1st level, you can communicate simple ideas with beasts through sounds and gestures and understand the intentions by interpreting their body language and grunts, growls, chirps, or other sounds.
4280 You also know the secret Druidic language. You can speak the language and use it to leave hidden messages. You and others who know this language automatically spot such a message. Others spot the message's presence with a successful DC 15 Wisdom (Perception) check but can't decipher it without magic.
4281 Hidebound
4282 Also at 1st level, you know the ancient secrets of protecting yourself with simple animal hides, instead of tempered metal. For you, hide armor sets your armor class to 10 + your Wisdom modifier + your Dexterity modifier (max 2).
4283 When you reach level 6 in this class, you can add 3, rather than 2, to your AC while wearing hide armor if you have a Dexterity of 16 or higher.
4284 When you reach level 17, choose one from bludgeoning, piercing, or slashing damage. You have resistance to that damage type while wearing light or hide armor.
4285 Heart of the Wilderness
4286 At 2nd level, you claim the limb of an ancient tree or a piece of ancient stone or obsidian and fashion it into a simple melee weapon, a shortbow, or a longbow. You can use your Wisdom modifier, instead of Strength or Dexterity, for attack and damage rolls with the weapon you create through this feature.
4287 The weapon created through this feature functions as an ordinary weapon of its type when wielded by anyone other than you.
4288 When you reach level 5 in this class, the damage die for the weapon created through this feature increases to 1d6 if it is smaller, and it counts as magical for the purpose of overcoming damage resistances. At level 9, it increases to1d8, or to 1d10 for a melee weapon wielded with two hands. At level 13, it increases to 1d10, or 2d6 for a melee weapon wielded with 2 hands. At level 17, it increases to 1d12, or 2d8for a melee weapon wielded with 2 hands.
4289 You cannot claim a second Heart of the Wilderness unless the first is destroyed or irretrievably lost (as determined by the DM). If you lose your Heart of the Wilderness, you can spend 24 hours seeking out an appropriate tree and fashioning a replacement.
4290 Nature Expert
4291 Also at 2nd level, choose one skill from Animal Handling, Nature, or Survival. You gain proficiency in that skill if you don't have it. If you are already proficient, you can add double your proficiency bonus to any check you make in the chosen skill.
4292 Shamanic Tradition
4293 At 3rd level, you may pick from the Land-Bound Shaman, the Medicine Man, the Star Caller, and the Witch Doctor traditions.
4294 Sky Reader
4295 Also at 3rd level, you know how to read the winds and clouds; you have a general idea of what the weather in your area will be for the next 24 hours as long as you can see the sky. This includes whether it will rain or snow and how severely.
4296 Ability Score Improvement
4297 When you reach 10th level in this class, you can increase one ability score of your choice by 2, or you can increase two ability scores of your choice by 1. As normal, you can’t increase an ability score above 20 using this feature.
4298 Land's Stride
4299 Also at 4th level, moving through nonmagical difficult terrain costs you no extra movement.
4300 Extra Attack
4301 Beginning at 5th level, you can attack twice, instead of once, whenever you take the Attack action on your turn.
4302 Wilderness Haven
4303 Starting at 7th level, you have learned how to create hidden shelters within the wilderness. If you spend 30 minutes preparing a site before a long rest, you can create a hidden camp that provides shelter and protection from the elements for you and a number of creatures up to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of 1). The camp is an area with a maximum radius of 20 feet.
4304 While taking a long rest in this site, you and your companions are not detectable to any outside creatures, even if you light a small fire. A suspicious creature can notice the camp if they pass a Wisdom (Perception) check (DC = 5 +your Wisdom modifier + your Shaman level), and any creature notices the camp if you or one of your companions leave the camp, attack a creature outside it, or force it to make a saving throw.
4305 The camp is also well-protected against the elements, keeping you and your companions warm and dry in all but the most extreme weather.
4306 At the end of your long rest, the camp breaks down and leaves no sign that you and your companions rested there.
4307 Twin Hearts
4308 At 11th level, you can claim a second branch of a sacred tree, or a suitable piece of sacred stone or obsidian.
4309 You can fashion this into a wooden shield or another weapon from those listed under the Heart of the Wilderness feature. If you choose a shield, you gain a +1 bonus to your armor class and saving throws while you are wielding it.
4310 Your secondary Heart functions as an ordinary shield or weapon of its type when wielded by anyone other than you.
4311 Like your original Heart of the Wilderness, you cannot claim another secondary Heart unless the original is destroyed or irretrievably lost (as determined by the DM), and the process to replace it is the same.
4312 Language of the Birds
4313 By 13th level, you've learned to call on small birds to carry messages for you. If you are in the wilderness, you can callout in the language of the birds as an action. If you are not in combat or an obviously dangerous location, a Tiny bird will answer your call and arrive at your location within 10minutes. You can tie a message to the bird's leg (the bird can carry nothing heavier than a message of up to 100 words written on paper or parchment), specify a location, which you must have visited, and a recipient who matches a general description, such as "a man or woman dressed in the uniform of the town guard" or "a red-haired dwarf wearing a pointed hat."
4314 The bird travels for up to 7 days, covering about 50 miles per 24 hours, attempting to reach the specified location and recipient. When the bird arrives, it gains the attention of the intended recipient, who receives the message unless they are incapacitated or otherwise unable. If the messenger doesn't reach its destination in time or cannot find the intended target (because they are dead, no longer match the given description, or for any other reason), the message is lost and the bird makes its way back to where you first called it.
4315 Once you have used this ability to send a message, you cannot do so again until you finish a long rest.
4316 Natural Resilience
4317 At 15th level, you gain the hardiness required to live in the harshest conditions. You gain proficiency in Constitution saving throws.
4318 Speaker for the Land
4319 At 17th level, you are able to learn the lay of the land from the faintest stirring of the wind, traces of animal tracks, and other signs too subtle for the uninitiated to perceive. By spending an uninterrupted minute focusing your senses on the various signs of the land, you gain knowledge of up to three facts of your choice about any of the following subjects as they relate to the area within 3 miles of you if you are outdoors, or 1000feet if you are underground:
4320 terrain and bodies of water
4321 prevalent plants, minerals, animals, or peoples
4322 powerful beasts, dragons, elementals, fey, monstrosities, plants, or undead
4323 influence from other planes of existence
4324 buildings
4325 For example, you could determine the location of powerful undead in the area, the location of major sources of safe drinking water, and the location of any nearby towns.
4326 This ability fails if you are in an area where nature has been replaced by construction, such as in dungeons and towns.
4327 Once you have used this ability, you cannot do so again until you finish a long rest.
4328 Nature's Vigor
4329 By 18th level, you have endured harsh environments, terrible storms, and brutal combat. You can draw on great reserves of vitality your experience in the natural world has granted you in times of peril. As a bonus action when you have fewer than half of your hit points remaining, you can regain a number of hit points equal to half your hit point maximum.
4330 Once you use this feature, you can’t use it again until you finish a long rest.
4331 Sage of the Wilds
4332 By 20th level, you have learned the secrets of the natural world and know how to defend yourself from the many creatures that lurk within. You have advantage on saving throws against the spells and abilities of beasts, plants, fey, and elementals.
4333 Shamanic Traditions
4334 The Land-Bound Shaman
4335 Denizen of the Land
4336 Variant: Elemental HeartWhen you gain the Heart of the Land feature, it is possible that your Heart of the Wilderness takes on properties related to the terrain type chosen through the Denizen of the land feature. A Heart of the Wilderness claimed from the frozen peak of a mountain or eternally frozen glacier in the tundra might deal cold damage, for example.Under this variant rule, your Heart of the Wilderness deals additional damage of the same type that your Denizen of the Landfeature granted you advantage on saving throws against, instead of dealing additional damage of its type.This variant rule may break the low-magic theme of the rest of the class by giving easy access to elemental damage, so be sure to discuss with your DM whether it is suitable for your campaign.Variant: Elemental HeartWhen you gain the Heart of the Land feature, it is possible that your Heart of the Wilderness takes on properties related to the terrain type chosen through the Denizen of the land feature. A Heart of the Wilderness claimed from the frozen peak of a mountain or eternally frozen glacier in the tundra might deal cold damage, for example.Under this variant rule, your Heart of the Wilderness deals additional damage of the same type that your Denizen of the Landfeature granted you advantage on saving throws against, instead of dealing additional damage of its type.This variant rule may break the low-magic theme of the rest of the class by giving easy access to elemental damage, so be sure to discuss with your DM whether it is suitable for your campaign.At 3rd level, you are a true resident of nature. Choose one of the types of terrain listed below. You gain the benefits listed under that terrain. You always have those benefits, even when not in your chosen terrain. While in your chosen terrain, you have advantage on initiative rolls, Intelligence (Nature)checks, and Wisdom (Survival) checks if you are not wearing heavy armor.
4337 Variant: Elemental Heart
4338 When you gain the Heart of the Land feature, it is possible that your Heart of the Wilderness takes on properties related to the terrain type chosen through the Denizen of the land feature. A Heart of the Wilderness claimed from the frozen peak of a mountain or eternally frozen glacier in the tundra might deal cold damage, for example.
4339 Under this variant rule, your Heart of the Wilderness deals additional damage of the same type that your Denizen of the Land
4340 feature granted you advantage on saving throws against, instead of dealing additional damage of its type.
4341 This variant rule may break the low-magic theme of the rest of the class by giving easy access to elemental damage, so be sure to discuss with your DM whether it is suitable for your campaign.
4343 Variant: Elemental Heart
4344 When you gain the Heart of the Land feature, it is possible that your Heart of the Wilderness takes on properties related to the terrain type chosen through the Denizen of the land feature. A Heart of the Wilderness claimed from the frozen peak of a mountain or eternally frozen glacier in the tundra might deal cold damage, for example.
4345 Under this variant rule, your Heart of the Wilderness deals additional damage of the same type that your Denizen of the Land
4346 feature granted you advantage on saving throws against, instead of dealing additional damage of its type.
4347 This variant rule may break the low-magic theme of the rest of the class by giving easy access to elemental damage, so be sure to discuss with your DM whether it is suitable for your campaign.
4349 Arctic: You have advantage on saving throws against effects that deal cold damage or are caused by extreme cold and require half as much food as an ordinary person.
4350 Coast/Sea: You have advantage on saving throws against effects that deal lightning damage and gain a swim speed equal to your walking speed.
4351 Desert: You have advantage on saving throws against effects that deal fire damage or are caused by extreme heat and require half as much water as an ordinary person.
4352 Forest/Jungle: You have advantage on saving throws against poison and gain a climb speed equal to your walking speed.
4353 Mountain: You have advantage on saving throws against effects that deal cold damage and gain a climb speed equal to your walking speed.
4354 Plains/Grasslands: You have advantage on saving throws against effects that deal lightning damage and your walking speed increases by 10 feet.
4355 Underground/Underdark: You have advantage on saving throws against poison and gain darkvision to a range of 30feet. If you have darkvision, its range increases by 10 feet.
4356 Swamp: You have advantage on saving throws against effects that deal acid damage and gain a swim speed equal to your walking speed.
4357 Heart of the Land
4358 Also at 3rd level, your bond with the land of its origin allows you to wield your Heart of the Wilderness to its full potential. Your Heart of the Wilderness deals an additional 1d6 damage of its type on a hit if it is a melee weapon, or 1d4 for a ranged weapon. When you reach level 9 in this class, this increases to 1d8 for melee or 1d6 for ranged. When you reach level 14,it increases to 1d10 for melee or 1d8 for ranged.
4359 Festival of the Land
4360 At 6th level, you gain an ability that celebrates an aspect of nature, often one associated with the terrain type chosen through the Denizen of the Land feature. You gain one of the following abilities of your choice:
4361 Festival of Renewal: You celebrate life and renewal. Whenever you or an ally you can see regains hit points or gains temporary hit points, you can use your reaction to grant yourself and one ally of your choice that can see or hear you temporary hit points equal to your Wisdom modifier + your proficiency bonus.
4362 Festival of Storms: You celebrate the fury of tempest and sandstorm. Whenever you score a critical hit or reduce a creature to 0 hit points with a melee weapon attack on your turn, you can use a bonus action to move up to half your speed and make one additional attack with that weapon.
4363 Festival of Vitality: You celebrate the ability to survive even the harshest of environments. While you are conscious but your current hit points are below half your hit point total, you gain temporary hit points equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of 1) at the start of each of your turns.
4364 Harvest Festival: You celebrate the reaping of one life to nourish another. When you reduce a creature to 0 hit points, you gain temporary hit points equal to your Shaman level, or your Shaman level + your Wisdom modifier if you used your Heart of the Wilderness to strike the killing blow.
4365 One with the Land
4366 Also at 6th level, you have grown resistant to the common dangers of the land you are from, can move through it unseen, and notice the slightest changes in that environment. You have advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks and Wisdom (Perception) checks while in the terrain you chose through the Denizen of the Land feature.
4367 You also gain resistance to a damage type associated with the terrain type you chose through the Denizen of the land feature, as listed below:
4368 Arctic: Cold damage.
4369 Coast/Sea: Lightning damage.
4370 Desert: Fire damage.
4371 Forest/Jungle: Poison damage.
4372 Mountain: Cold damage.
4373 Plains/Grassland: Lightning damage.
4374 Underground/Underdark: Poison damage.
4375 Swamp: Acid damage.
4376 You always have this damage resistance, even if you are not int he terrain you chose through the Denizen of the Land feature.
4377 Strength of the Land
4378 By 9th level, you are rooted to the land and draw strength from it. While standing on natural ground, if you would be knocked prone, shoved, or moved against your will, you can use your reaction to remain standing or remain in place.
4379 While in the terrain you chose through the Denizen of the Land feature, you are also considered proficient in Strength saving throws.
4380 Master of Many Lands
4381 By 14th level, you can see the connections between all lands and nature, though your connection to your homeland remains the strongest. You automatically succeed on Intelligence (Nature) checks related to the terrain you chose through the Denizen of the Land feature and on Wisdom (Survival) checks while you are in it.
4382 When you take damage of the type you gained resistance to through the Resilience of the Land feature, you can use your reaction to reduce the damage taken to 0. You can do this a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of1) and regain all uses when you finish a long rest.
4383 In addition, whenever you finish a short or long rest in any of the terrain types listed under the Denizen of the Land feature, you gain the benefits of the Denizen of the Land feature (but not the One with the Land feature) for that terrain type. This lasts until you leave that terrain.
4384 The Medicine Man
4385 Evil Eye
4386 At 3rd level, you know how to exploit the fears of the superstitious with a mere gaze. As a bonus action, you can look into the eyes of a humanoid that you can see that is within 30 feet of you and can see you, forcing them to make a Wisdom saving throw (DC = 8 + your proficiency bonus +your Wisdom modifier). On a failure, the target is frightened of you for one minute. A frightened creature can repeat the save at the end of each of their turns, ending the effect on a success. A creature that saves against this ability is immune to it for 24 hours.
4387 Additionally, people find it hard to meet your gaze. Insight checks made to learn your true thoughts or intentions are made with disadvantage.
4388 Healing Incense
4389 Also at 3rd level, you have learned the ancient medicinal traditions of your ancestors. You gain proficiency with the craft (Herbal) and Medicine skill, and your proficiency bonus is doubled for any check you make with them.
4390 If you have access to an Herbalism Kit, you can create and burn small doses of healing incense during a short rest. Any willing creature that remains within 60 feet of you for the short rest's duration can inhale the incense. A creature (including you) that inhaled the incense during the rest and rolls a value on any expended hit dice lower than your Wisdom modifier can use your Wisdom modifier as the die result instead.
4391 When you reach level 9 in this class, a creature that inhales your healing incense for the duration of a short rest also gains temporary hit points equal to half your Shaman level +your Wisdom modifier.
4392 When you reach level 14 in this class, a creature that inhales your healing incense for the duration of a short rest has their levels of exhaustion, if any, reduced by one. Once this ability has reduced a creature's level of exhaustion, it cannot do so again for that creature until they finish a long rest.
4393 Healing Salve
4394 At 6th level, if you have access to an Herbalism Kit during along rest, you can create 3 potions of greater healing as part of that long rest.
4395 When you reach level 9 in this class, potions created by this feature become potions of superior healing.
4396 When you reach level 14 in this class, potions created through this feature become potions of supreme healing.
4397 Potions created through this feature are unstable and cannot be sold. They lose potency at the end of your next long rest.
4398 Herbal Remedy
4399 Also at 6th level, you are able to quickly create cures for common ailments. If you have access to an herbalism kit, you can use an action to cure a willing creature within 5 feet of you of one of the following conditions: blinded, deafened, paralyzed, or poisoned.
4400 You can use this ability a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of 1), and regain all uses when you finish a long rest.
4401 Healer's Rebuke
4402 By 9th level, you can rebuke the weak hearted with your hardened stare. When a creature within 30 feet of you that you can see, that can see you, and that is frightened of you due to your Evil Eye ability attempts to make an attack or force a creature to make a saving throw, you can use your reaction to force that creature to make a Wisdom saving throw (DC = 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Wisdom modifier). On a failure, the attack, ability, or spell fails and is wasted.
4403 If the creature fails the saving throw by 5 or more, it cannot make another attack or force another creature to make another saving throw until the start of its next turn.
4404 Herbal Restoration
4405 At 14th level, you have learned secret herb lore that allows you to overcome the deadliest of afflictions and even discovered rare herbs that might turn back death itself.
4406 By spending 10 minutes treating a creature within 5 feet of you, you can cure them of petrification or one effect that is reducing one of their ability scores or their maximum hit points. If the creature you are treating moves or you are ever more than 5 feet away from them during the duration, the treatment fails. Whether the treatment succeeds or fails, the treated creature gains one level of exhaustion.
4407 In addition, you can use an action to restore one creature to life if their body is within 5 feet of you and they have died within the last minute. They return to life with one hit point, and their levels of exhaustion, if any, are reduced by one. A creature must complete a short or long rest before it can benefit from this ability again.
4408 The Star Caller
4409 Star Channeler
4410 Choose two of the stances listed below. As a bonus action, you can enter one of your chosen stances, gaining the benefits listed below:
4411 Beast: When you take this stance and as a bonus action one each of your turns while in it, you can make one melee weapon attack. Each time you make this special attack, the first attack against you before the start of your next turn has advantage on the attack roll.
4412 Chariot: While in this stance, your movement speed increases by 5 feet, opportunity attacks against you are made at disadvantage, and if you move at least 20 feet toward a target before hitting it with a melee weapon attack, you can roll one of the weapon's damage dice one additional time and add it to the damage dealt.
4413 Guardian: While in this stance, creatures within 5 feet of you have disadvantage on attack rolls against creatures other than you.
4414 Hunter: When you enter this stance and as a bonus action on each turn while in it, you can focus on a single target you can see. Until the end of the turn, your ranged weapon attacks against that target don't suffer from disadvantage if they are within the long range of your weapon, and they deal an additional 1d4 damage on a hit.
4415 Warrior: While in this stance, you gain a +1 bonus to melee weapon attack rolls, melee weapon damage rolls, and armor class.
4416 A stance lasts until you are incapacitated, you enter another stance, combat ends, or you choose to end it as a bonus action. You can enter a stance a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of 1), and regain all uses when you finish a long rest.
4417 Celestial Navigation
4418 At 3rd level, you can easily navigate by your knowledge of celestial bodies. While you can see the sky, you always know which way is north, and you and those traveling with you can’t become lost except by magical means.
4419 Constellation Expert
4420 At 6th level, your knowledge of the constellations expands. You learn an additional stance from those listed under the Star Channeler feature, and if you roll initiative with no uses of your Star Channeler ability remaining, you regain one use.
4421 Lunar Phases
4422 At 6th level, your study of the moon has allowed you to learn a skill corresponding to one of its major phases. You learn one the following skills of your choice:
4423 Crescent Moon: You can add your Wisdom modifier to initiative rolls and dim light doesn't impose disadvantage on your Wisdom (Perception) checks.
4424 Full Moon: You cannot be surprised if you are conscious, and while in bright light, you can see up to 1 mile away with no difficulty, able to discern even fine details as though looking at something no more than 100 feet away from you.
4425 New Moon: Creatures don't gain advantage on attack rolls against you as a result of being unseen by you, and while you are in darkness, you have advantage on Dexterity(Stealth) checks.
4426 Solstice and Equinox
4427 By 9th level, your study of the solar path has allowed you to learn a skill associated with one of its major yearly milestones. You gain one of the features of your choice detailed below:
4428 Autumn Equinox: When you are hit by an attack that is not acritical hit, you can use your reaction to halve the damage of the attack against you.
4429 Spring Equinox: Whenever you roll an attack roll, ability check, or saving throw in which you are proficient and roll a value below a 10 but not a 1 on the die, you can use your reaction to treat the die roll as a 10 instead. You must use this ability before knowing the result of the roll.
4430 Summer Solstice: Whenever you score a critical hit with a weapon, you can roll one of the weapon's damage die two additional times, or three if the weapon is your Heart of the Wilderness, and add the total to the damage dealt. When you are hit by a critical hit by a melee weapon attack, you can expend a use of this ability and use your reaction to turn the critical hit into a normal hit.
4431 Winter Solstice: When you roll a 1 on an attack roll, an ability check, or a saving throw, you can reroll the d20 and must use the new result. When an enemy rolls a 1 on an attack roll against you, you can expend a use of this ability and use your reaction to make a weapon attack against them if they are within your reach.
4432 You can use the ability provided by this feature a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of once), and regain all uses when you finish a long rest. If you roll initiative and have no uses of this ability remaining, you regain one use.
4433 Master of Constellations
4434 By 14th level, you can perfectly act out each of your people's stories of the constellations. You learn an additional stance rom those listed under the Star Caller feature, and whenever you enter a stance, you gain the additional benefits listed below:
4435 Beast: When you enter this stance, and each time you make a weapon attack as a bonus action while in it, you gain temporary hit points equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum 1).
4436 Chariot: While in this stance, your movement speed increases by an additional 5 feet, and if you move at least 20 feet toward a Large or smaller creature before hitting it with a melee weapon attack, you can force it to make a Strength saving throw (DC = 8 + your Wisdom modifier +your proficiency bonus) or be knocked prone.
4437 Guardian: While in this stance, you can use your reaction to make a melee weapon attack against any creature within 5feet of you that attacks a creature other than you. On a hit, your target cannot attack the creature it targeted with the triggering attack again until the end of the current turn.
4438 Hunter: When you focus on a target while in this stance, your ranged weapon attacks against that target ignore half and three quarters cover and reduce the target's movement speed by 5 feet until the start of your next turn on a hit. This effect can stack up to 3 times.
4439 Warrior: While in this stance, the bonus to your armor class increases to +3, and you can add your proficiency bonus to the damage of your melee weapon attacks.
4440 The Sword Medium
4441 Heart of the Sword
4442 When you choose this tradition at 3rd level, you gain proficiency in Performance, and with short swords and longswords. Over the course of an hour, you can reforge your Heart of the Wilderness into a scimitar, a short sword, or a longsword. This is usually a special obsidian blade or a weapon forged in the flames of your old Heart of the Wilderness. This new weapon counts as your Heart of the Wilderness and gains all its benefits while you wield it, but serves as an ordinary weapon of its type for anyone else. When you gain the Twin Hearts feature at level 11, you can choose to create another scimitar, short sword, or long sword.
4443 In addition, you are trained in sacred dance. Whenever you make a Charisma (Performance) check related to dance, you can add your Wisdom modifier to that check.
4444 Sword Arts
4445 Also at 3rd level, you are trained in the art of the sword. You gain one of the following fighting styles of your choice:
4446 Dueling: When you are wielding a melee weapon in one hand and no other weapons, you gain a +2 bonus to damage rolls with that weapon.
4447 Great Weapon Fighting: When you roll a 1 or 2 on a damage die for an attack you make with a melee weapon you are wielding with two hands, you can re-roll the die and must use the new roll, even if the new roll is a 1 or a 2. The weapon must have the two-handed or versatile property for you to gain this benefit.
4448 Two Weapon Fighting: When you engage in two-weapon fighting, you can add your ability modifier to the damage of the second attack.
4449 Sword Channeler
4450 At 6th level, you are able to redirect the forces of nature through your sword. While wielding your Heart of the Wilderness, you can use your reaction when you take acid, cold, fire, lightning, or thunder damage to gain resistance to the triggering damage. The first time before the end of your next turn that you hit a creature with your Heart of the Wilderness, you deal an additional 1d6 damage of the triggering type for every 5 damage you took (minimum of 1d6), to a maximum of 10d6 damage. If you were dealt 53 fire damage, for example, and used this feature to reduce the damage taken to 26, you would deal an additional 5d6 fire damage on the first hit with your Heart of the Wilderness before the end of your next turn. You can use this ability a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of1) and regain all uses when you finish a long rest.
4451 Sacred Dance
4452 Also at 6th level, you learn a sacred dance that allows you to weave through the battlefield with deadly grace. You gain one of the following features of your choice:
4453 Ancestral Dance:  Your dance is inspired by the great feats of your ancestors. You learn 2 maneuvers from the Battle Master list. If a maneuver you use requires your target to make a saving throw to resist its effects, the saving throw DC equals 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Wisdom modifier. You gain 3 superiority dice, d6s, which can be used to fuel your maneuvers. A superiority die is expended when you use it. You regain your expended superiority dice when you roll initiative or finish a short or long rest.
4454 Dance of the Avalanche: Your dance is inspired by the unstoppable avalanche. When you take the attack action and hit the same creature with two melee attacks as part of that action, you can make one additional attack against the same creature as a bonus action.
4455 Dance of the Spreading Flame: Your dance is inspired by raging fires. Attacks with your Heart of the Wilderness score a critical hit on a roll of 19 or 20, and once on each of your turns when you score a critical hit or reduce a creature to 0 hit points with a weapon attack, you can make another attack with the same weapon against a different creature that is within 5 feet of the original target and within range of your weapon.
4456 Dance of the Tides: Your dance is inspired by the ebb and flow of the tides. Opportunity attacks against you are made with disadvantage, and once per turn when you enter melee range with a creature for the first time, you can use your bonus action to make an attack with your Heart of the Wilderness against them.
4457 Dance of the Whirlwind: Your dance is inspired by the furious whirlwind. Each time you hit a creature with a melee attack with your Heart of the Wilderness, you can deal damage of the same type equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of 1) to each other creature of your choice within 5 feet of you.
4458 Sword Exorcism
4459 By 9th level, your blade is anathema to unnatural forces. Each time that you hit an aberration, fiend, or undead with your Heart of the Wilderness, you deal an additional 1d6 damage of your weapon's type.
4460 In addition, if a creature is possessed by another entity, you can use your action to make a single attack against that creature with your Heart of the Wilderness. On a hit, you deal damage to the possessor instead of the possessed creature, and the possessor must make a Charisma saving throw (DC= 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Wisdom modifier). On a failure, the possession immediately ends, and the possessor cannot posses another creature for 24 hours. On a successful saving throw, the possessor is immune to this ability for 24hours.
4461 Sword Sage
4462 By 14th level, you have mastered the blade. While wielding your Heart of the Wilderness, the fighting style you chose through the Sword Arts feature is enhanced as listed below:
4463 Dueling: When you are wielding a melee weapon in one hand and no other weapons, you gain a +5 bonus to damage rolls with that weapon, instead of +2.
4464 Great Weapon Fighting: When you roll a 1 or 2 on a damage die for an attack you make with a melee weapon you are wielding with two hands, you can treat the die roll as the maximum value for that die, instead of rerolling it.
4465 Two Weapon Fighting: When you engage in two-weapon fighting, you can add your ability modifier to the damage of the second attack. In addition, you can make a second attack with your offhand weapon when you engage in two weapon fighting.
4466 The Witch Doctor
4467 Poisonous Blood
4468 When you choose this tradition at 3rd level, you consume secret concoctions that temper your body and make your blood poisonous to anyone but yourself. While not wearing armor, your Armor Class is now equal to 10 + your Wisdom Modifier + your Dexterity modifier. You can wield a shield and still gain this benefit. When you reach level 17 in this class, you gain the damage resistance granted by your Hidebound feature even when you are not wearing armor.
4469 Additionally, when a creature within 5 feet of you hits you with a melee attack that deals slashing or piercing damage, you can use your reaction to force your attacker to make a Constitution saving throw (DC = 8 + your Wisdom modifier +your proficiency bonus). The attacker takes 2d8 poison or acid damage (your choice) on a fail, or half as much on a success. You can use this ability a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of once) and regain all uses when you finish a long rest.
4470 Poisonous Brew
4471 Also at 3rd level, you have learned the art of brewing ancient and secret poisons. You gain proficiency in Craft (Poison) and can add double your proficiency bonus to any checks you make with it. If you have access to a Poisoner's Kit during a long rest, you can prepare a number of special vials of poison equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of 1) as part of the rest. A creature can use the poison in each vial to coat one slashing or piercing melee weapon they are wielding or up to five pieces of ammunition as a bonus action. You can also apply this poison to your Heart of the Wilderness, even if it is a weapon that deals bludgeoning damage. Once applied, the poison retains potency for 1 minute before drying.
4472 The first time on a turn that a creature is hit by the poisoned weapon or ammunition, it takes an additional 1d8 poison damage.
4473 Poisons created through this feature are unstable and cannot be sold. They lose potency at the end of your next long rest.
4474 Mithridatism
4475 By 6th level, you have consumed small doses of various types of poisons to build up a tolerance to them. You have advantage on saving throws against poison, gain resistance to poison damage, and whenever you roll initiative and have no uses of your Poisonous Blood ability remaining, you regain one use.
4476 Poisonous Dust
4477 Also at 6th level, you learn to create small amounts of poisonous dust. Once per turn when you take the Attack action, you can forgo one of your attacks to toss or blow a pinch of this poisonous dust at a creature within 10 feet of you. The target and each creature within 5 feet of them other than you must make a Constitution saving throw. On a failure, a creature takes a number of d8s of poison damage equal to your proficiency bonus and is poisoned for one minute. It can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on a success. On a success, a target takes half as much damage and isn't poisoned.
4478 You can use this ability a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of once) and regain all uses when you finish a long rest.
4479 Poison Savant
4480 By 9th level, you have become a master of the poisoner's art. You gain the following benefits:
4481 You can add your Wisdom modifier to poison damage dealt by your Poisonous Blood, Poisonous Brew and Poisonous Dust features.
4482 Poison damage dealt by your Poisonous Blood, Poisonous Brew and Poisonous Dust features ignore resistance to poison damage.
4483 When you use your Poisonous Dust feature, its range is increased to 20 feet, you can choose to affect creatures within 10 feet of the initial target, and creatures that fail their initial saving throws are blinded until the end of their next turn.
4484 Devourer of Poisons
4485 By 14th level, you have become so steeped in poisons that they no longer affect you. You are immune to poison damage and the poisoned condition, damage dealt by your Poisonous Blood feature increases by 2d8, and poison damage dealt through your Shaman features deals half damage to creatures that are normally immune to poison.
4486 Whenever you would take poison damage from a hostile creature, you can use your reaction to regain hit points equal to half the poison damage you would have taken (ignoring your immunity and any resistances) and regain one use of your Poisonous Blood ability if you have expended any.
4487 You can use this reaction a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of once) and regain all uses when you finish a long rest.

4616 Hit Points Hit Dice: 1d8 per Minstrel level.
4617 Proficiencies Armor: Light armor
4618 Weapons: Simple weapons, hand crossbows, longswords, rapiers, shortswords
4619 Saving Throws: Dexterity, Charisma
4620 Skills: Performance and any other three.
4621 Equipment
4622 (a) a rapier, (b) a longsword, or (c) any simple weapon
4623 Adventure pack, Ink, pen and 10 sheets of paper
4624 (a) a lute or (b) any other musical instrument
4625 Leather armor and a dagger
4626 Chants
4627 Starting at 1st level, you can sing special songs called Chants, which imbue your allies with courage and strength or demoralize your enemies. You know 2 Chants at level 1 and learn additional Chants as you gain levels in this class, as shown on the Chants known section of the Minstrel table. The Chants available to you are detailed at the end of the class description. If a Chant has a level requirement, you must reach that level in this class before being able to learn it. In addition, whenever you gain a level in this class, you can replace a Chant you know with another. You can begin singing or playing a Chant as a bonus action on your turn, which lasts for 1 minute, until you are incapacitated, or until you choose to end it (no action required). You can sing a number of Chants equal to your Charisma modifier (minimum of 1) and regain all uses when you finish a long rest.
4628 Jack of All Trades
4629 Starting at 2nd level, you can add half your proficiency bonus, rounded down, to any ability check you make that doesn’t already include your proficiency bonus.
4630 Song of Rest
4631 Beginning at 2nd level, you can use soothing music or oration to help revitalize your wounded allies during a short rest. If you or any friendly creatures who can hear your performance regain hit points at the end of the short rest by spending one or more Hit Dice, each of those creatures regains an extra 1d6 hit points. The extra hit points increase when you reach certain levels in this class: to 1d8 at 9th level, to 1d10 at 13th level, and to 1d12 at 17th level.
4632 Minstrel Path
4633 At 3rd level, you choose a path to further refine your Minstrel skills. You may pick from the Troubador, Skald, Sword Dancer, and Snake Charmer paths. Each path is detailed at the end of the class description. You gain features based on your path at 3rd, 7th, 11th, and 15th levels.
4634 Expertise
4635 At 3rd level, choose two of your skill proficiencies. Your proficiency bonus is doubled for any ability check you make that uses either of the chosen proficiencies. At 10th level, you can choose another two skill proficiencies to gain this benefit.
4636 Ability Score Improvement
4637 When you reach 4th level, and again at 8th, 10th, 12th, 16th, and 19th level, you can increase one ability score of your choice by 2, or you can increase two ability scores of your choice by 1. As normal, you can’t increase an ability score above 20 using this feature.
4638 Extra Attack
4639 Beginning at 5th level, you can attack twice, instead of once, whenever you take the Attack action on your turn.
4640 Rondo
4641 By 6th level, you have learned to extend your Chants. Any Chant you sing now lasts for 10 minutes. A Chant still ends early if you are incapacitated or you choose to end it (no action required). In addition, if you roll initiative and have no uses of your Chant remaining, you regain one use.
4642 Swan Song
4643 Starting at 9th level, you learn to end your Chant in a mighty crescendo to grant yourself a burst of strength. When you hit with a weapon attack while singing a Chant, you can choose to end that Chant (no action required). If you do, the weapon attack deals additional damage equal to twice your Minstrel level and you cannot begin singing another Chant for one minute.
4644 Minstrel Ability score Improvement
4645 10th level, you can increase one ability score of your choice by 2, or you can increase two ability scores of your choice by 1. As normal, you can't increase an ability score above 20 using this feature.
4646 Overwhelming Performance
4647 By 13th level, you've learned to drown out sounds that might interfere with your Chant. While you are singing a Chant, you and each creature of your choice within range of the Chant's effect has resistance to thunder damage.
4648 Silver Tongue
4649 By 14th level, your way with words is beyond compare. When you make a Charisma check, you can choose to take a 15 on the die roll, instead of rolling. You must choose to use this ability before the die roll. You can use this ability a number of times equal to your Charisma modifier (a minimum of once), and regain all uses when you finish a long rest.
4650 Overture
4651 By 17th level, you've learned to preface your Chants with a rousing prelude. When you begin a Chant, each ally within its range has advantage on attack rolls, saving throws, and ability checks until the start of your next turn. You must finish a short or long rest before you can use this ability again.
4652 Vibrato
4653 At 18th level, your Chants and other Minstrel abilities can affect even creatures that cannot hear your performance as the vibrations of your music spread throughout their bodies. When you begin singing a Chant, you can force each hostile creature in its range to make a Constitution saving throw (DC = 8 + your Charisma modifier + your proficiency bonus). On a failure, a creature has disadvantage on attack rolls for the Chant's duration as your song's vibrations disorients them. An affected creature can repeat the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on a success.
4654 Golden Voice
4655 At 20th level, your experience performing Chants has caused your voice to become naturally compelling. Your Charisma score increases by 4, as does your maximum for that score. Minstrel Paths Minstrels practice a diverse set of skills. At some point, however, any experienced minstrel chooses some of their abilities to master above all others. When doing so, they look to the paths laid down by the minstrels of legends that came before.
4657 Minstrel paths
4658 Path of the Troubador
4659 Resounding Song
4660 At 3rd level, you improve your mastery of Chants and song. You gain proficiency in Performance. If you are already proficient in Performance, you can choose one other skill instead. In addition, you are able to project the effects of your Chants to a greater range. Your Chants now affect creatures within 60 feet of you, instead of 30.
4661 Enthralling Performance
4662 Also at 3rd level, your performance has become so refined that you can affect people's emotions with ease. While you are performing a Chant, you can target one creature that can hear you and is within the Chant's range as a bonus action. The target must make a Wisdom saving throw (DC = 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Charisma modifier). On a failure, it is charmed or frightened by you (your choice) and you have advantage on attack rolls against it until your Chant ends. An affected creature can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on a success. You can use this ability a number of times equal to your Charisma modifier (a minimum of once), and regain all expended uses when you finish a short or long rest.
4663 Soothing Note
4664 At 7th level, you've learned to add calming notes to your Chants that discourage enemies from attacking you. Whenever a creature within 60 feet of you that can hear you targets you with an attack while you are singing a Chant, you can use your reaction to force the attacker to make a Wisdom saving throw (DC = 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Charisma modifier). A creature that succeeds on its saving throw against this ability is immune to it for 1 hour. On a failed save, the attacker must choose a new target or lose the attack, and cannot target you with an attack until the start of its next turn.
4665 Polyphony
4666 By 11th level, your understanding of Chants is unmatched. Whenever you begin singing a Chant, you can now choose to grant the effects of two Chants that you know. This only expends one use of your Chant ability.
4667 Captive Audience
4668 By 15th level, your performances have become so captivating that some who hear them can do nothing but stand in awe. Whenever a creature fails its saving throw against your Enthralling Performance, its movement speed is reduced to 0 for as long as it is charmed or frightened by you. If a creature fails its initial save against your Enthralling Performance by 5 or more, you can choose for it to be stunned for the duration, instead of charmed or frightened.
4670 Path of the Skald
4671 Training in Sword and Song
4672 By 3rd level, you've trained to master the weapons of a warrior as well as the musical arts for which all Minstrels are known. You gain proficiency with martial weapons, medium armor, and shields.
4673 Rhythm of Battle
4674 Also at 3rd level, you learn to blend the rhythms of your war chants with your martial prowess. While singing a Chant, you gain a +1 bonus to weapon attack rolls and the benefits of one of the fighting styles listed below for the Chant's duration. You gain no benefit if you have already gained that fighting style from another source. You can choose to adopt a different style each time you begin to sing a Chant.
4675 Defense While you are wearing armor, you gain a +1 bonus to AC.
4676 Dueling When you are wielding a melee weapon in one hand and no other weapons, you gain a +2 bonus to damage rolls with that weapon.
4677 Great Weapon Fighting When you roll a 1 or 2 on a damage die for an attack you make with a melee weapon you are wielding with two hands, you can re-roll the die and must use the new roll, even if the new roll is a 1 or a 2. The weapon must have the two-handed or versatile property for you to gain this benefit.
4678 Protection When a creature you can see attacks a target other than you that is within 5 feet of you, you can use your reaction to impose disadvantage on the attack roll. You must be wielding a shield.
4679 Thrown Weapon Fighting You can draw a weapon that has the thrown property as part of the attack you make with the weapon. In addition, when you hit with a ranged attack using a thrown weapon, you gain a +2 bonus to the damage roll.
4680 Two Weapon Fighting When you engage in two-weapon fighting, you can add your ability modifier to the damage of the second attack.
4681 March to My Beat
4682 By 7th level, you've learned to shape the tempo of your Chant to the chaos of the battlefield, and by adding the right note at the right time, can spur an ally to additional action or throw off an enemy's timing. Once each turn while you are singing a Chant, you can use a bonus action or forgo one of your attacks when you take the Attack action to target either an ally or an enemy that can hear you and is within 30 feet of you. If you target an ally, they can immediately use their reaction to either make a single melee weapon attack against a target within reach or move up to half their speed without provoking opportunity attacks. If you target an enemy, they must make a Wisdom saving throw (DC 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Charisma modifier) or immediately use their reaction to make a single melee weapon attack against a target of your choice within their reach. If there are no targets within their reach or the targeted creature cannot make a melee weapon attack for any reason (such as being incapacitated), the ability fails and is wasted. You can use this ability a number of times equal to your Charisma modifier (a minimum of once) and regain all uses after a short or long rest.
4683 Rhythm of War
4684 At 11th level, you have mastered the ability to blend steel and song into a deadly dance. Whenever you use your Rhythm of Battle ability to gain a fighting style, the effects of that fighting style are improved in the manner listed below. If you have gained any fighting styles from any other source, they are not improved by this ability.
4685 Defense While you are wearing armor, you gain a +3 bonus to AC, instead of +1.
4686 Dueling When you are wielding a melee weapon in one hand and no other weapons, you gain a +5 bonus to damage rolls with that weapon, instead of +2.
4687 Great Weapon Fighting When you roll a 1 or 2 on a damage die for an attack you make with a melee weapon you are wielding with two hands, you can treat the die roll as the maximum value for that die, instead of rerolling it.
4688 Protection When a creature you can see attacks a target other than you that is within 5 feet of you, you can use your reaction to impose disadvantage on the attack roll. Until the creature you have protected is no longer within 5 feet of you or the start of your next turn, all attacks against that creature have disadvantage.
4689 Thrown Weapon Fighting You can draw a weapon that has the thrown property as part of the attack you make with the weapon. In addition, when you hit with a ranged attack using a thrown weapon, you gain a +3 bonus to the damage roll, instead of +2, and the weapon immediately returns to your hand after it hits or misses its target.
4690 Two Weapon Fighting When you engage in two-weapon fighting, you can add your ability modifier to the damage of the second attack. In addition, you can make a second attack with your offhand weapon when you engage in two weapon fighting.
4691 Legend of War and Song
4692 By 15th level, you have integrated your fighting style with your war chants to true perfection. Whenever you begin singing a Chant, you can choose to adopt two fighting styles from the Rhythm of Battle list. Both of these fighting styles receive the improvement granted by your Rhythm of War.
4694 Path of the Sword Dancer
4695 Graceful Dance
4696 At 3rd level, you learn to use dance rather than song to empower your allies. When you would begin singing a Chant, you can choose to channel the Chant through a dance if you are not wearing medium or heavy armor or wielding a shield. If you do, a creature does not need to be able to hear to be affected by the Chant. Instead, they must be able to see you. Your dance ends early if you are incapacitated, if you don medium or heavy armor or a shield, or if you use two hands to make an attack with a weapon. While dancing in this way, your movement speed increases by 10 feet and standing up from prone takes only 5 feet of your movement speed. In addition, your armor class becomes 10 + your Dexterity modifier + you Charisma modifier, if it is not higher.
4697 Weapon Dance
4698 Also at 3rd level, you choose a weapon through which you can channel your dance. This must be a melee weapon that lacks the two-handed, heavy, and special properties. You gain proficiency with the weapon if you do not have it already, and the weapon's damage die increases to a d8 for you if it is smaller. You can use your Dexterity modifier for attack and damage rolls with your chosen weapon. You have advantage on Charisma (Performance) checks involving dance if you are wielding your chosen weapon.
4699 Dazzling Displays
4700 At 7th level, you learn to use your dance to keep your enemies off balance and exploit their openings. Once on each of your turns when you hit an enemy with a melee weapon attack using the weapon you chose through your Weapon Dance feature, you can perform one of the following displays. If a display requires the target to make a saving throw, the DC is equal to 8 + your Charisma modifier + your proficiency bonus. If you are channeling a Chant through your dance, the attack also deals additional damage equal to your Charisma modifier when you perform a display.
4701 Dizzying Display The target must make a Wisdom saving throw or have disadvantage on attack rolls until the end of your next turn.
4702 Fierce Display The target must make a Strength saving throw or be pushed up to 10 feet in a direction of your choice. It must end this movement in an unoccupied space.
4703 Tumbling Display The target must make a Dexterity saving throw or be knocked prone.
4704 Whirling Display The target and each other creature of your choice within 5 feet of you take damage equal to one roll of your weapon's damage die.
4705 You can perform a number of displays equal to your Charisma modifier (a minimum of 1) and regain all uses when you finish a short or long rest.
4706 Deadly Dance
4707 By 11th level, your mastery of your weapon dance makes your every strike more deadly. While channeling a Chant through your dance, the damage die for the weapon chosen with your Weapon Dance feature increases to 1d12, and you score a critical hit with it on a roll of 19 or 20.
4708 Triumphant Dance
4709 By 15th level, your dance has become a marvel of grace and lethality. While channeling a Chant through your dance, you deal an additional 1d12 damage the first time each turn you hit with a weapon attack using the weapon you chose through your Weapon Dance feature. In addition, if you have expended any uses of your Dazzling Display ability, you regain one use of it whenever you score a critical hit with your chosen weapon.
4711 Path of the Snake Charmer
4712 Charmed Animal
4713 At 3rd level, you can call forth an animal that you have charmed, which serves as your partner and companion. The animal is friendly to you and your companions and obeys your commands.
4714 Choose its stat block — The Serpent, The Monkey, or The Falcon — which uses your proficiency bonus (PB) and Charisma bonus (CB) in several places. In combat, the animal acts during your turn. It can move and use its reaction on its own, but the only action it takes is the Dodge action, unless you take a bonus action on your turn or sacrifice one of your attacks when you take the Attack action to command it to take another action.
4715 You can also command your charmed animal to act as part of the same bonus action that you use to begin singing a Chant. That action can be one in its stat block or some other action. If you are incapacitated, the beast can take any action of its choice, not just Dodge. Your charmed animal can only take the Help action to aid you or another ally with a skill check if it can add your proficiency bonus to that skill check through its Exceptional Training feature.
4716 If the animal dies and you do not have the means to revive it, you must seek out and train a new animal that can serve as your companion. While some animals, such as trained falcons, might be available for purchase in some locations, it is more likely that you will need to spend an extended period of time searching for, befriending, and training another animal. Consult with your DM on how this will affect you character and the campaign.
4717 If you die, the animal returns to the wilds or takes some other appropriate action determined by the DM.
4718 The Serpent Small beast Armor Class 14 + PB (natural armor) Hit Points 5 + five times your minstrel level (the Serpent has a number of Hit Dice [d8s] equal to your minstrel level) Speed 30 ft., swim 30 ft. STR DEX CON INT WIS CHA 9 (-1) 16 (+3) 14 (+2) 5 (-3) 15 (+2) 14 (+2) Senses Blindsight 10 ft., passive Perception 12 + PB Languages understands one language you speak Proficiency Bonus (PB) equals your bonus CB equals your Charisma bonus.
4719 Venomous: If the Serpent scores a critical hit with a bite attack, the creature that was bitten is poisoned for 1 minute. A creature affected by this poison can make a Constitution saving throw (DC = 8 + PB + CB) at the end of each of its turns, ending the poisoned condition on a success.
4720 Exceptional Training: You can add your proficiency bonus to any saving throw that the Serpent makes, as well as any Intimidation, Performance, Perception, Stealth, or Survival check it makes.
4721 Actions: Bite. Melee Weapon Attack: CB + PB to hit, range 5 ft., one target. Hit: 1d4 + 3 piercing damage + 1d4 + PB poison damage.
4722 The Monkey (Feylar) Small beast Armor Class 13 + PB (natural armor) Hit Points 5 + five times your minstrel level (the Monkey has a number of Hit Dice [d8s] equal to your minstrel level) Speed 30 ft., climb 30 ft. STR DEX CON INT WIS CHA 12 (+1) 14 (+2) 16 (+3) 8 (-1) 12 (+1) 12 (+1) Senses passive Perception 11 + PB Languages understands one language you speak Proficiency Bonus (PB) equals your bonus CB equals your Charisma bonus
4723 Monkey See, Monkey Do: The Monkey can wield any simple melee weapon with the Light property, using CB + PB for its bonus to hit and adding its Strength modifier to damage rolls, or its Dexterity modifier if the weapon also has the Finesse property. It cannot wield any other type of weapon.
4724 Exceptional Training: You can add your proficiency bonus to any saving throw that the Monkey makes, as well as any Acrobatics, Investigation, Performance, Perception, or Sleight of Hand check it makes.
4725 Actions:
4726 Fist. Melee Weapon Attack: CB + PB to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 1d6 + 2 + PB bludgeoning damage.
4727 Rock. Ranged Weapon Attack: CB + PB to hit, range 25/50 ft., one target. Hit: 1d4 + 2 + PB bludgeoning damage.
4728 The Falcon (Kes’trekel) Small beast Armor Class 13 + PB (natural armor) Hit Points 4 + four times your minstrel level (the Falcon has a number of Hit Dice [d6s] equal to your minstrel level) Speed 10 ft., fly 60 ft. STR DEX CON INT WIS CHA 6 (-2) 16 (+3) 12 (+1) 5 (-3) 14 (+2) 12 (+1) Senses Darkvision 60 ft., passive Perception 17 + PB Languages understands one language you speak Proficiency Bonus (PB) equals your bonus WB equals your Wisdom bonus Flyby. The falcon doesn't provoke opportunity attacks when it flies out of an enemy's reach.
4729 Keen Sight: The falcon has advantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on sight.
4730 Exceptional Training: You can add your proficiency bonus to any saving throw that the falcon makes, as well as any Acrobatics, Performance, Perception, or Survival check it makes.
4731 Actions Shred: Melee Weapon Attack: CB + PB to hit, range 5 ft., one target. Hit: 1d4 + 3 + PB slashing damage.
4732 Charmer of Beasts
4733 Also at 3rd level, you gain proficiency in Animal Handling. If you are already proficient, you can add double your proficiency bonus whenever you make a Wisdom (Animal Handling) check. Your charmed animal can add double your proficiency bonus to any Charisma (Performance) check it makes while you are playing an instrument, issuing commands, or otherwise accompanying its performance.
4734 You also learn the Chant of Animal Tranquility. If you already know it, you learn one other Chant of your choice. This does not count against your number of Chant known, and beasts that you have not attacked within the last 24 hours make their saving throw against your Chant of Animal Tranquility with disadvantage.
4735 Charmer of Monsters
4736 At 7th level, your song can soothe the most savage of beasts. When you play or sing the Chant of Animal Tranquility, it can affect creatures within 60 feet of you, you can choose to have it last for up to an hour, and it can affect any Dragon, Monstrosity, or Elemental that hears it and is within its range if it has an Intelligence of 5 or lower.
4737 When you reach level 11 in this class, your Chant of Animal Tranquility can affect creatures of these types with 10 Intelligence or lower. When you reach level 15 in this class, your Chant of Animal Tranquility can affect creatures of these types regardless of their Intelligence score, but creatures with 11 or greater Intelligence have advantage on their saving throws against the Chant's effects. In addition, creatures charmed by your Chant of Animal Tranquility will follow simple commands that you give them, such as "follow", "flee", or "stay" (no action required). They will not fight for you or obey a command that leads them into obvious danger, and they will cease to obey your commands if they are attacked, forced to make a saving throw, take any damage, the charmed condition ends, or your Chant ends.
4738 Tandem Dance
4739 By 11th level, you and your charmed animal can move in perfect harmony to the rhythm of your Chants. While you are singing or playing a Chant, your charmed animal's movement speed increases by 10 feet. It also gains a +2 bonus to its armor class, has advantage on Dexterity saving throws, and can attack twice, instead of once, when you order it to take the Attack action.
4740 Charmer of Men
4741 By 15th level, your hypnotic song can even affect humanoids. Your Chant of Animal Tranquility can now affect humanoids, though humanoids with an Intelligence score of 11 or greater have advantage on their saving throws against its effects. In addition, you can now order beasts and other creatures with an Intelligence of 5 or lower under the effect of your Chant of Animal Tranquility to attack a target of your choice (no action required), and beasts and other creatures with an Intelligence score of 5 or lower no longer immediately cease to obey your commands if they are attacked, forced to make a saving throw, or take any damage. Instead, they can make a Wisdom saving throw (DC = 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Charisma modifier) each time they take damage, ending the Chant's effect on a success.
4743 Chants
4744 Unless otherwise stated, a Chant can only effect creatures that can hear and are within 30 feet of the performer. If a Chant requires a creature to make a saving throw, the DC is equal to 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Charisma modifier.
4745 Chant of Accuracy.
4746 This lively tune inspires clarity and surehandedness. While it is in effect, your allies can roll a d4 whenever they make an attack roll and add it to the result.
4747 Chant of Action (requires level 17).
4748 This legendary Chant inspires allies to the greatest heights of valor. While it is in effect, each ally can take an additional action on each of their turns. This action can be used only to take the Dash, Disengage, Hide, or Use an Object Action, or to make a single weapon attack. A creature that benefits from this Chant gains one level of exhaustion when the effect ends.
4749 Chant of Alacrity.
4750 This Chant grants the swiftness of the wind to those that hear it. While you sing this Chant, the movement speed of your allies increases by 5 feet, and they ignore difficult terrain. When you reach level 11 in this class, their movement speed increases by 10 feet, and they can take the Dash action as a bonus action.
4751 Chant of Animal Tranquility
4752 This soothing Chant causes animals to become docile in the performer's presence. Any beast that starts its turn within range of you and is able to hear this Chant must make a Wisdom save or be charmed by you until the Chant ends. If you or an ally attack an affected beast or force it to make a saving throw, the charm ends, and it is immune to this Chant for the next 24 hours.
4753 Chant of Defense
4754 This quick-tempoed Chant helps to sharpen the senses and allows allies to react more quickly to danger. Whenever an affected ally is hit by an attack, they can use their reaction to add your Charisma bonus to their Armor Class, potentially turning the hit into a miss.
4755 Chant of Devotion (requires level 9)
4756 This song prevents those who hear it from having their minds clouded. While it is in effect, your allies are immune to the charmed condition.
4757 Chant of Freedom
4758 This song awakens the love of freedom in all who hear it. While it is in effect, your allies have advantage on saving throws against effects that would paralyze or restrain them, as well as on ability checks to escape a grapple.
4759 Chant of Might
4760 This passionate Chant inspires strength of arm. While it is in effect, your allies can add your Charisma modifier to the damage roll the first time they hit a creature with a weapon attack on each of their turns.
4761 Chant of Pain (requires level 5)
4762 This song is filled with discordant notes and screeches that are anathema to those hostile to the performer. While it is in effect, each hostile creature takes psychic damage equal to your proficiency bonus at the start of each of their turns.
4763 Chant of Protection (requires level 11)
4764 This rhythmic song inspires steadiness and confidence in allies. While it is in effect, any bludgeoning, piercing, or slashing damage your allies take from non-magical weapons is reduced by an amount equal to your Charisma modifier. This does not stack with the Heavy Armor Master feat or any other ability that similarly reduces these damage types.
4765 Chant of Resistance (requires level 7)
4766 This uplifting Chant inspires allies to stand in the face of any adversity. Your allies can add your Charisma bonus to any saving throw they make while it is in effect.
4767 Chant of Sanctity (requires level 11)
4768 This sacred Chant inspires allies to struggle on, even in the face of death. While it is in effect, when an ally is subjected to an effect that would kill it instantaneously without dealing damage, that effect is negated. In addition, if an ally is reduced to 0 hit points by taking damage, it is reduced to 1 hit point instead. Once an ally has benefited from either of this Chant's effects, they cannot benefit from this Chant again until they finish a short or long rest.
4769 Chant of Skill
4770 This harmonious Chant inspires your allies with confidence in their abilities. While it is in effect, your allies can roll a d4 whenever they make a skill check and add it to the result.
4771 Chant of Truth (requires level 17)
4772 This song tells of how all things were meant to be at the beginning of time. While it is in effect, your allies gain truesight and can see into the Ethereal plane to a range of 60 feet.
4773 Chant of Valor (requires level 7)
4774 This song of ancient heroes inspires fearlessness in all who hear it. While it is in effect, your allies are immune to the frightened condition.
4775 Chant of Vigor
4776 This vibrant melody energizes your companions in battle. While you perform it, your allies gain temporary hit points equal to your proficiency bonus at the start of each of their turns.
4777 Chant of Vulnerability (requires level 11)
4778 This dolorous melody shakes confidence and inspires frailty. While it is in effect, any creature hostile to you must make a Wisdom saving throw at the start of their turn. On a failure, they are stripped of any damage resistances and damage immunities they have until the start of their next turn. On a success, they are immune to this Chant's effects for 24 hours.
4779 Requiem
4780 Any undead creature that starts its turn within range of you while this Chant is in effect takes radiant damage equal to your Charisma modifier. When you reach 11th level in this class, this damage is doubled. When you reach level 17, it is tripled.
4781 Wayward Chant (requires level 5)
4782 This haunting song demoralizes the enemies of its performer, causing their blows to go astray. While it is in effect, all creatures hostile to you and your allies must roll a d4 each time they make an attack roll and subtract it from the total.
